Text:"Look Only When Needed: Certificate-Guided Visual Routing for Efficient Diffusion VLMs Donghyeon Cho1,*, Joowon Kim1,*, Seungho Shin1 and Eunho Yang1,2, 1KAIST, 2AITRICS *Equal Contribution, Corresponding Author Diffusion vision-language models (DVLMs) process their entire input at every denoising step. Since visual tokens make up 8997% of this input on the benchmarks we study, most of the decoding computation is spent repeatedly processing the same image. Visual token pruning methods partially relieve this burden by removing part of the visual tokens, but they lose the removed information and still process the remaining image at every step. To measure how often the image is actually needed, we compare LLaDA-Vs predictions with and without the image at every decoding step and find that removing the image changes token being unmasked at only 22.8% of steps on average and at as few as 7.9%. We therefore propose LOWN, decoding method that decides at every step whether to process the image and how many tokens to unmask. LOWN predicts the masked tokens without the image and processes the image only when this prediction disagrees with the last image-conditioned prediction. Agreement certifies the earlier prediction for reuse, and the number of certified tokens sets how many are unmasked in that step. We further observe that the need for the image differs across benchmarks, so LOWNs adaptive decision is needed rather than fixed schedule. Without training or tuned threshold, LOWN retains 99.6% of LLaDA-Vs score on ten benchmarks at 35.3% of the decoding FLOPs on average. LOWN also generalizes across LLaDA-based DVLMs, composes orthogonally with visual token pruning, and runs under key-value caching for further efficiency gains. Code and project page are available at https://hyeon9698.github.io/LOWN/ 1. Introduction Recent diffusion language models match similarly sized autoregressive models while refining several masked positions in parallel over denoising steps (Nie et al.,"

Text:"A-RSI: Adaptive Task Refresh for Zero-Data Multimodal Self-Improvement Yu Liu1,4,* Youxing Li2,*, Yuedong Tan3,* Zhiwei Yang1,4 Zihan Su5 Hanqi Feng6 Shaojie Zhang4 Libin Liao9 Zihao Wan9 Chenxu Wu9 Cong Cao1, Fangfang Yuan1 Yanbing Liu1 Ziheng Chen7,8 Pei Fu4, Radu Timofte3 Zongwei Wu3 Nicu Sebe8 Barnabas Poczos6 Jian Luan4 1 *Equal contribution Project lead Corresponding authors 2 6 7 3 4 9 Abstract Recursive self-improvement requires models not only to acquire useful training experience, but also to decide when their current experience has ceased to be useful. Existing methods often focus on what experience to generate next, while paying less attention to when fresh experience should be introduced. Many also construct training targets through self-consistency or majority voting, making supervision depend on the models own uncertain predictions. We address these two questions separately: training dynamics determine when to acquire new experience, while the answers to new tasks remain independently checkable. A-RSI monitors reward variation and accuracy in training logs to refresh task pool when its learning signal declines, without consulting validation or test scores. static library of model-authored programs produces multimodal questions and derives their reference answers; we specify the task-generation contract, automatically verify chosen properties of the reward and refresh rules, and separately audit the generated answers. The training loop uses no external training examples or human-written answer labels. On the primary eleven-benchmark suite, A-RSI raises the performance average from 57.77 to 61.05 with 800 fresh tasks. Figure 1 Motivation for A-RSI. (a) Pool reuse can stall learning, while fewer truncations can raise accuracy. Text:"Read, Allocate, Route: Role-Coupled Tokenization for Pixel-Space Generative Transformers Jiongfan Yang1,3, Weichen Fan2, Haiwen Diao2, Zhongang Cai3, Ziwei Liu2, 1Peking University, 2Nanyang Technological University, Corresponding author 3SenseTime, Pixel-space generative Transformers typically use one token per image patch, so capturing finer detail requires more tokens and makes global attention increasingly expensive. We observe that finer detail does not necessarily require more global communication: low-frequency structure benefits from global exchange, while high-frequency detail can be modeled locally. Based on this insight, we introduce SpecSlot, pixel-space rectified-flow Transformer that decouples output detail from the length of the global attention sequence. SpecSlot expands each spatial anchor into multiple slots, each assigned disjoint band of DCT coefficients; this separates anchor density from spectral subdivision. Only the lowest-frequency slot from each anchor participates in global attention, while all slots interact locally. This allows the model to increase spectral detail without enlarging the global sequence. spatial head further adds local correction to the spectral prediction, while the generative state, target, and loss remain in pixel space. On ImageNet-256, SpecSlot achieves comparable final FID to FLOPs-matched JiT control while converging faster. On ImageNet-512, it improves FID by 2.84 over the control with 3.9% fewer FLOPs, and comes within only 0.69 FID of JiT-B/16 at 38.2% lower cost. On text-to-image generation, SpecSlot surpasses JiT-B/8 on DPG-Bench by 1.06 at 40.6% lower forward cost. Text:"2026-10-30 AB-Attention: Accurate Block Attention for Accelerating Video Diffusion Transformers Bin Xu1,2 Mo Zhang3 Zhengfeiyang Zhang1,4 Xinfeng Yang5 Yixing Ma1,6 Wei Wang7 HeXin Wang1 Pengfei Zhou1,8 Wangbo Zhao7 1Inf Rec 4The University of Tokyo 5Fudan University 2The Chinese University of Hong Kong, Shenzhen 3Shandong University 6University of California, Berkeley 7The Hong Kong University of Science and Technology 8National University of Singapore Corresponding authors Abstract Video diffusion transformers generate high-quality videos, but the quadratic cost of 3D full attention dominates their inference. Dynamic block-sparse attention reduces this cost by computing exact attention only on key/value blocks selected from current activations. However, its fidelity degrades under aggressive sparsity: blocks are scored from coarse summaries or formed by costly global clustering, pruned blocks vanish from the softmax, and whole tiles are kept or dropped even when only few of their keys matter. To address these limitations, we propose Accurate Block Attention (AB-Attention), training-free method that accurately approximates dense attention at high sparsity. Its Intra-Block Clustering Selector (IBCS) estimates block importance faithfully yet cheaply by clustering queries only within each query block, without permuting tokens. Sampling with Inclusion-Probability Weighting (SIP) then samples blocks in proportion to these estimates and reweights them by inverse inclusion probability, so that one pass estimates dense attention rather than truncating it. Finally, its kernel gathers key/value blocks much smaller than tile into full tensor-core tiles, refining selection at no extra tensor-core cost. On Wan2.1 and HunyuanVideo, AB-Attention skips 8992% of attention, whereas the baselines compute 1.83.6 as much, yet it still improves PSNR by up to 4.4 dB over the strongest baseline and achieves up to 2.23 end-to-end speedup. Our code is available at https: //github.com/InfRec"

Text:"Multi-Token Prediction Done Right Abstract Multi-Token Prediction (MTP) is now common ingredient in language-model pre-training, as it improves downstream performance and enables faster inference through speculative decoding. We identify flaw in standard MTP training, in which residual component of the MTP gradient interferes with both next-token and multi-token learning, increasingly so with scale. Specifically, the MTP cross-entropy gradient can be decomposed exactly into KL self-distillation term that pulls each MTP head toward the models own next-token prediction (NTP) distribution and improves speculative decoding, and residual that has low signal-to-noise ratio and buries the useful KL signal. We introduce the KL gradientrecovery trick: when two distributions share linear output head, the gradient of their KL divergence can be recovered exactly by combining their cross-entropy gradients through straight-through gradient routing. Unlike minimizing the KL directly, this trick requires no vocabulary-sized logit matrices and is compatible with standard fused cross-entropy kernels. This enables Soft Multi-Token Prediction (Soft-MTP), one-line code change to MTP that removes the noisy residual and backpropagates only the KL self-distillation signal by reusing the existing NTP and MTP cross-entropy computations. Soft-MTP supports both DeepSeekand Meta-style MTP, adds no parameters, and incurs no measurable overhead. From 167M to 1.2B parameters, Soft-MTP improves over standard MTP with lower NTP perplexity, faster speculative decoding and higher zero-shot accuracy at every scale, lower MTP perplexity from 689M onward, and stronger math and code reasoning after fine-tuning at 1.2B. Soft-MTP also matches, if not exceeds, direct KL divergence minimization on most metrics. Soft-MTP is, in effect, free lunch for MTP optimization. Our code can be found at https://github.com/JaydenTeoh/Soft-MTP. Soft-MTP illustration One-line code change Loss performance = shared_trunk(x) h"

Text:"Multi-Token Prediction Done RightAbstract Multi-Token Prediction (MTP) is now common ingredient in language-model pre-training, as it improves downstream performance and enables faster inference through speculative decoding. We identify flaw in standard MTP training, in which residual component of the MTP gradient interferes with both next-token and multi-token learning, increasingly so with scale. Specifically, the MTP cross-entropy gradient can be decomposed exactly into KL self-distillation term that pulls each MTP head toward the models own next-token prediction (NTP) distribution and improves speculative decoding, and residual that has low signal-to-noise ratio and buries the useful KL signal. We introduce the KL gradientrecovery trick: when two distributions share linear output head, the gradient of their KL divergence can be recovered exactly by combining their cross-entropy gradients through straight-through gradient routing. Unlike minimizing the KL directly, this trick requires no vocabulary-sized logit matrices and is compatible with standard fused cross-entropy kernels. This enables Soft Multi-Token Prediction (Soft-MTP), one-line code change to MTP that removes the noisy residual and backpropagates only the KL self-distillation signal by reusing the existing NTP and MTP cross-entropy computations. Soft-MTP supports both DeepSeekand Meta-style MTP, adds no parameters, and incurs no measurable overhead. From 167M to 1.2B parameters, Soft-MTP improves over standard MTP with lower NTP perplexity, faster speculative decoding and higher zero-shot accuracy at every scale, lower MTP perplexity from 689M onward, and stronger math and code reasoning after fine-tuning at 1.2B. Soft-MTP also matches, if not exceeds, direct KL divergence minimization on most metrics. Soft-MTP is, in effect, free lunch for MTP optimization. Our code can be found at https://github.com/JaydenTeoh/Soft-MTP. Soft-MTP illustration One-line code change Loss performance = shared_trunk(x) h0 = ntp_head(z) for in range(1, K+1): hk = mtp_head[k-1](z, x) loss_mtp += ce(lm_head, hk, y[k]) # straight-through gradient routing # h0 is numerically unchanged h0[:, k:] += hk.detach() - hk # NTP loss and gradients are unchanged loss_ntp = ce(lm_head, h0, y[0]) # -ve gradient from NTP loss enters hk # this recovers KL gradients (loss_ntp + loss_mtp).backward() Figure 1: Soft-MTPs illustration, pseudocode, and loss performance compared to MTP. Blue line is the one-line code change. We refer readers to Algorithm 1 for the full code implementation of Soft-MTP, including the MTP loss weighting. Correspondence to: jayden_t@mit.eduNext-token prediction (NTP) has become general recipe for training large language models (LLMs), yielding models with remarkable capabilities across wide range of tasks [Radford et al., 2019]. One might argue that the NTP objective is sufficient any distribution over token sequences can, in theory, be factorized autoregressively and modeled by sufficiently expressive next-token predictor [Sutskever, 2023]. Yet, Multi-Token Prediction (MTP) [Stern et al., 2018, Qi et al., 2020, Gloeckle et al., 2024], which trains model to predict multiple future tokens, has now been widely adopted in modern LLM training, including in DeepSeek-V3/4 [DeepSeek-AI, 2024, 2026a], Nemotron 3 [NVIDIA, 2025], GLM-5 [GLM-5-Team, 2026], and Kimi K3 [Kimi Team, 2026], just to name few. MTP offers two main advantages over NTPbetter model performance and more efficient inference. As learning objective, MTP has been shown to alleviate the myopic nature of the next-token supervision [Bachmann and Nagarajan, 2024, Gloeckle et al., 2024, Nagarajan et al., 2025, Huang et al., 2026], leading to stronger downstream performance on long-horizon domains such as mathematics and code. More importantly, MTP heads can also be used for speculative decoding [Leviathan et al., 2022, Chen et al., 2023] to accelerate model inference. Despite the wide adoption of MTP, we still understand little about how it interacts with next-token prediction and which objective is best for training the MTP heads. During speculative decoding, token drafted by MTP is accepted only if it agrees with the models own next-token prediction. If the goal is to improve speculative decoding, shouldnt we directly train the models MTP heads to match its own NTP distribution via self-distillation? Recent work does exactly this [Teoh et al., 2025, Zhao et al., 2026a], but naïvely minimizing an explicit KL divergence requires materializing vocabulary-sized logit tensors for both the teacher and the student, which is costly in memory and incompatible with the standard fused cross-entropy kernels used in modern pre-training [Hsu et al., 2025, Wijmans et al., 2025]. In practice, frontier models still train their MTP heads with standard cross-entropy against the data. In this paper, we show that the standard MTP cross-entropy gradient already contains this self-distillation signal. The MTP gradient decomposes exactly into KL term gKL, which pulls the MTP head toward the models own next-token distribution, and residual gres, which is the NTP cross-entropy gradient against the data (see Section 4). We provide evidence that this residual in the MTP loss gradient has low signal-to-noise ratio (SNR) and interferes with both NTP and MTP learning, especially as scale increases. We then introduce the KL gradientrecovery trick: when two distributions share linear output head, the gradient of their KL divergence can be recovered exactly from their cross-entropy gradients through straight-through gradient routing [Bengio et al., 2013, van den Oord et al., 2017], without explicitly computing the KL or materializing any logits. This trick enables Soft Multi-Token Prediction (Soft-MTP), which removes the residual and sends only the useful KL self-distillation gradient through the MTP heads and the shared model trunk, with one-line code change (see Figure 1). From 167M to 1.2B parameters, Soft-MTP improves over standard MTP with lower NTP perplexity, faster self-speculative decoding in every domain we test, and higher zero-shot accuracy at every scale, as well as stronger code and math reasoning after fine-tuning. It also reaches lower MTP loss from 689M parameters onward. Soft-MTP matches or exceeds the costly explicit KL self-distillation on most metrics, at no extra cost over standard MTP. Soft-MTP supports both DeepSeek-style [DeepSeek-AI, 2024] and Meta-style [Gloeckle et al., 2024] MTP "

Text:"After One-Click Video: The Inflow of Service-Type Content, the Failure of the Recommendation Pool Mechanism, and the BoLe System Abstract In September 2026, new large language model was released. Soon after, new way of making explainer videos became very popular: people asked the model to write program, and the program produced the video. One-click video content then appeared in large amounts on many platforms. On the surface, this is rise in creative power at the level of the individual. We argue that, at deeper level, it changes the structure of the content industry. This judgment rests on two observations. First, this wave of content is service-type content. People watch it to meet need, not to enjoy the content itself. The other kind is work-type content. AI makes both kinds faster to produce. But anyone can come in and make service-type content, while work-type content is limited by the number of people who have something to say. So the ratio between the two slowly becomes unbalanced. Second, free platforms choose content through layers of trial viewing, and this uses up user attention. The total amount of attention is fixed. The unbalanced ratio also dilutes good content. simplified mathematical model shows that, as long as these conditions hold, the recommendation pool mechanism will fail once new content grows past certain multiple. Based on this, we discuss one possible way out. Machines and entry rules judge whether service-type content passes the bar. Trusted people judge work-type content. We call this the BoLe system. At the end, we briefly imagine how the two kinds of content may develop from here. Using machines to mass-produce content in exchange for traffic and ad revenue share is not new. At the end, we briefly imagine how the two kinds of content may develop from here.Using machines to mass-produce content in exchange for traffic and ad revenue share is not new. More than ten years ago, content farms had already turned choose topics by ad price, write cheaply from templates into the business of listed company (Roth, 2009). In autumn 2026, new turning point came. Opus 5.5 was released on September 22. After that, new method spread fast on social platforms: use the model to write program that draws each frame and puts the frames together into motion graphics video. These videos do not go through video generation model. Instead, the model writes program that draws every frame, and the program is then rendered into video file (dSebastien, 2026). One week after the release, the industry newsletter Latent Space wrote in review that these code-generated explainer videos had taken over the timeline (Latent Space, 2026). Once the method was understood, other top-tier models could also produce similar results. Soon, one-click video skill packs and tutorials were everywhere on all platforms. The platforms have not published numbers for this kind of content. The scale of the wave is the authors observation as people who work in the field. The first author has direct experience of how fast this is. Our team has made professional short videos for more than ten years. In the past, five-minute science video built mainly on motion graphics took five days. We first made the effects frame by frame in After Effects, then edited and put it together in Final Cut Pro. Now, with the first author checking each video in person and taking responsibility for the content, we can make three to five videos in one day. Readers who study AI systems may not follow these changes in the content market. So we first explain them clearly. The next question is this: so many beautiful videos are pouring out, but what exactly are they?The large number of beautiful videos in this wave is, in essence, service, not content in the traditional sense. Their goal is to serve. People watch them to meet certain need, not to enjoy the work itself. Service-type content: Its goal is to meet certain need of the viewer. People watch it for that need. Example: most AI short dramas. Work-type content: People watch it to enjoy the content itself. Examples: long video in which big YouTube creator explains some event; Netflix series; Stephen King novel. The difference between the two is not production cost, and it is not length. one-click video and long video by big YouTube creator can sit in the same feed. They can play in the same player. Both can live on ad revenue share. Even if we make the two the same length and spend the same money on them, viewers still get two different things from them. Seeing these two kinds of content clearly helps several groups: the producers who are now pouring in, the platforms that run content, and the people who build AI creative tools and models. Which kind they are dealing with decides what they should value and how they should measure success. But the bigger question right now is this. Production efficiency has jumped by one to two orders of magnitude within few weeks. Much more service-type content than work-type content is pouring in. What will happen to the current content ecosystem?"

[30.10.2026 09:26] Claude request. Model: claude-haiku-5-5. Prompt: Read an abstract of the ML paper and return a JSON with fields: 'desc': explanation of the paper in Russian (4 sentences), use correct machine learning terms. 'emoji': emoji that will reflect the theme of an article somehow, only one emoji. 'title': a slogan of a main idea of the article in Russian. Return only JSON and nothing else.

Abstract Intrinsic Distribution Matching improves few-step text-to-image generation by adding explicit moment constraints to distribution matching distillation, achieving state-of-the-art long-text rendering with minimal network evaluations.  Generated by thinkingmachines/Inkling-Small One-step and few-step text-to-image generation must accurately render the details specified in the prompt despite limited opportunities for refinement. Distribution matching distillation (DMD) uses score differences to bring the generated distribution closer to a reference distribution. Following prior work, we estimate this difference with internal velocity branches, avoiding a separate fake-score network. We ask whether an explicit moment constraint improves this learned score supervision. A decomposition of the reverse-KL field into a Gaussian moment component and a non-Gaussian residual identifies statistics that can be constrained explicitly. We introduce Intrinsic Distribution Matching (IDM), which retains the full DMD correction and matches feature means and per-coordinate standard deviations using the generator's exponential-moving-average (EMA) copy, requiring no external feature encoder. Experiments use full-parameter and low-rank adaptation (LoRA) tuning on Z-Image-Turbo 6B and Qwen-Image 20B. At two network evaluations, IDM reaches 94.25% and 90.20% on Chinese and English LongText-Bench, the highest scores among the compared methods at this budget. Controlled ablations show gains from the added moment loss, while generator EMA features perform competitively with an external DINOv2 encoder across the five reported metrics. Code is available at https://github.com/inclusionAI/IDM.

Paper text to classify:

"Abstract Intrinsic Distribution Matching improves few-step text-to-image generation by adding explicit moment constraints to distribution matching distillation, achieving state-of-the-art long-text rendering with minimal network evaluations.  Generated by thinkingmachines/Inkling-Small One-step and few-step text-to-image generation must accurately render the details specified in the prompt despite limited opportunities for refinement. Distribution matching distillation (DMD) uses score differences to bring the generated distribution closer to a reference distribution. Following prior work, we estimate this difference with internal velocity branches, avoiding a separate fake-score network. We ask whether an explicit moment constraint improves this learned score supervision. A decomposition of the reverse-KL field into a Gaussian moment component and a non-Gaussian residual identifies statistics that can be constrained explicitly. We introduce Intrinsic Distribution Matching (IDM), which retains the full DMD correction and matches feature means and per-coordinate standard deviations using the generator's exponential-moving-average (EMA) copy, requiring no external feature encoder. Experiments use full-parameter and low-rank adaptation (LoRA) tuning on Z-Image-Turbo 6B and Qwen-Image 20B. At two network evaluations, IDM reaches 94.25% and 90.20% on Chinese and English LongText-Bench, the highest scores among the compared methods at this budget. Controlled ablations show gains from the added moment loss, while generator EMA features perform competitively with an external DINOv2 encoder across the five reported metrics. Code is available at https://github.com/inclusionAI/IDM."

Abstract This work formalizes benchmark evaluation through difficulty measures, identifies failure modes of measure mismatch and coverage gaps, and proposes diagnostic metrics and a rewriting algorithm to improve benchmark coverage.  Generated by thinkingmachines/Inkling-Small A benchmark is not merely a collection of test questions; it naturally induces an empirical measure over difficulty. In this work, we develop this measure-based view of benchmark evaluation. This perspective allows us to identify and formalize two central failure modes: measure mismatch, where the benchmark difficulty distribution differs from the intended difficulty distribution, and coverage gaps, where uncovered regions along the difficulty axis prevent benchmarks from detecting meaningful localized training progress. We propose two diagnostic metrics that capture these two failure modes and provide rigorous theoretical guarantees. Empirically, we analyze 25 popular benchmarks spanning 7 domains using carefully estimated item difficulties. Our results show that many benchmarks implicitly overweight certain difficulty regions, so their leaderboard conclusions should be interpreted with caution; others leave substantial portions of the difficulty axis uncovered, limiting their ability to monitor the model improvement process. Finally, we turn diagnosis into action by introducing BenchFill, a simple difficulty-targeted item rewriting algorithm that effectively reduces local coverage gaps.

Авторы выделяют два ключевых сбоя: несоответствие распределения сложности (measure mismatch) и пробелы в покрытии (coverage gaps), из-за которых бенчмарк не замечает локальный прогресс обучения. Предложены две диагностические метрики с теоретическими гарантиями, а также эмпирический анализ 25 популярных бенчмарков из 7 областей с оценкой сложности каждого элемента. Abstract LOWN adaptively skips image processing during diffusion vision-language model decoding when predictions agree, reducing computation while preserving accuracy.  Generated by thinkingmachines/Inkling-Small Diffusion vision-language models (DVLMs) process their entire input at every denoising step. Since visual tokens make up 89-97% of this input on the benchmarks we study, most of the decoding computation is spent repeatedly processing the same image. Visual token pruning methods partially relieve this burden by removing part of the visual tokens, but they lose the removed information and still process the remaining image at every step. To measure how often the image is actually needed, we compare LLaDA-V's predictions with and without the image at every decoding step and find that removing the image changes a token being unmasked at only 22.8% of steps on average and at as few as 7.9%. We therefore propose LOWN, a decoding method that decides at every step whether to process the image and how many tokens to unmask. LOWN predicts the masked tokens without the image and processes the image only when this prediction disagrees with the last image-conditioned prediction. Agreement certifies the earlier prediction for reuse, and the number of certified tokens sets how many are unmasked in that step. We further observe that the need for the image differs across benchmarks, so LOWN's adaptive decision is needed rather than a fixed schedule. Without training or a tuned threshold, LOWN retains 99.6% of LLaDA-V's score on ten benchmarks at 35.3% of the decoding FLOPs on average. LOWN also generalizes across LLaDA-based DVLMs, composes orthogonally with visual token pruning, and runs under key-value caching for further efficiency gains. Code and project page are available at https://hyeon9698.github.io/LOWN/
[30.10.2026 09:26] Response: {"desc": "Диффузионные vision-language модели (DVLM) на каждом шаге денойзинга обрабатывают всё входное изображение, хотя визуальные токены составляют до 89–97% входа, что делает декодирование очень затратным. Авторы показали, что удаление изображения меняет предсказание лишь примерно в 22,8% шагов, поэтому картинку нужно пересчитывать не всегда. Метод LOWN на каждом шаге сначала предсказывает маскированные токены без изображения и обращается к картинке только при расхождении с предыдущим предсказанием, а согласованные токены переиспользует и демаскирует. Без дополнительного обучения и подбора порогов метод сохраняет 99,6% качества LLaDA-V при использовании около 35% FLOPs на декодирование.", "emoji": "🖼️", "title": "Смотрим на картинку только тогда, когда это действительно нужно"}

Paper text to classify:

"Abstract LOWN adaptively skips image processing during diffusion vision-language model decoding when predictions agree, reducing computation while preserving accuracy.  Generated by thinkingmachines/Inkling-Small Diffusion vision-language models (DVLMs) process their entire input at every denoising step. Since visual tokens make up 89-97% of this input on the benchmarks we study, most of the decoding computation is spent repeatedly processing the same image. Visual token pruning methods partially relieve this burden by removing part of the visual tokens, but they lose the removed information and still process the remaining image at every step. To measure how often the image is actually needed, we compare LLaDA-V's predictions with and without the image at every decoding step and find that removing the image changes a token being unmasked at only 22.8% of steps on average and at as few as 7.9%. We therefore propose LOWN, a decoding method that decides at every step whether to process the image and how many tokens to unmask. LOWN predicts the masked tokens without the image and processes the image only when this prediction disagrees with the last image-conditioned prediction. Agreement certifies the earlier prediction for reuse, and the number of certified tokens sets how many are unmasked in that step. We further observe that the need for the image differs across benchmarks, so LOWN's adaptive decision is needed rather than a fixed schedule. Without training or a tuned threshold, LOWN retains 99.6% of LLaDA-V's score on ten benchmarks at 35.3% of the decoding FLOPs on average. LOWN also generalizes across LLaDA-based DVLMs, composes orthogonally with visual token pruning, and runs under key-value caching for further efficiency gains. Code and project page are available at https://hyeon9698.github.io/LOWN/"

Abstract A-RSI improves recursive self-improvement by refreshing training tasks based on declining learning signals and using independently verifiable program-generated answers, raising benchmark performance without external labels.  Generated by thinkingmachines/Inkling-Small Recursive self-improvement requires models not only to acquire useful training experience, but also to decide when their current experience has ceased to be useful. Existing methods often focus on what experience to generate next, while paying less attention to when fresh experience should be introduced. Many also construct training targets through self-consistency or majority voting, making supervision depend on the model's own uncertain predictions. We address these two questions separately: training dynamics determine when to acquire new experience, while the answers to new tasks remain independently checkable. A-RSI monitors reward variation and accuracy in training logs to refresh a task pool when its learning signal declines, without consulting validation or test scores. A static library of model-authored programs produces multimodal questions and derives their reference answers; we specify the task-generation contract, automatically verify chosen properties of the reward and refresh rules, and separately audit the generated answers. The training loop uses no external training examples or human-written answer labels. On the primary eleven-benchmark suite, A-RSI raises the performance average from 57.77 to 61.05 with 800 fresh tasks.
[30.10.2026 09:26] Response: {"desc": "В статье представлен метод A-RSI, который улучшает рекурсивное самосовершенствование моделей машинного обучения. Система отслеживает вариативность наград и точность в логах обучения и обновляет пул задач, когда обучающий сигнал падает, не обращаясь к валидационным или тестовым метрикам. Новые мультимодальные вопросы и эталонные ответы генерируются статичной библиотекой программ, созданных самой моделью, а ответы проверяются независимо. На наборе из одиннадцати бенчмарков средняя точность выросла с 57,77 до 61,05 при использовании 800 новых задач, без внешних размеченных данных.", "emoji": "🔄", "title": "Учиться дальше, когда старые задачи перестают работать"}

Abstract WorkFactory builds coherent professional tasks via information planning and repair, yielding WorkPilot models that improve expert-level agent performance across benchmarks.  Generated by thinkingmachines/Inkling-Small Scaling supervised training for working agents requires diverse professional tasks that yield reliable expert demonstrations. However, missing or inconsistent task information can obstruct teacher execution and weaken the resulting supervision. We propose WorkFactory, a scalable framework for constructing coherent and executable professional tasks. Starting from professional intents, information-level planning builds an information support graph to guide task instantiation, explicitly representing the dependencies and locations of information required for the intended outputs. Probe executions then identify support failures and guide repairs while preserving the intended work. We construct 112,686 tasks spanning 612 occupations and use the collected teacher trajectories to fine-tune Qwen models at 4B, 27B, and 35B-A3B scales, yielding the WorkPilot family. All three models improve over their respective base models across four professional-work benchmarks, with WorkPilot-35B-A3B improving from 879 to 1565 Elo on GDPval-AA v2.1. Scaling analyses reveal model-dependent diminishing returns, with execution length first increasing and then decreasing. Controlled component analyses show that planning and repair reduce probe-diagnosed task environment defects and improve downstream performance. The data and models are available at https://github.com/IQuestLab/WorkFactory.
[30.10.2026 09:26] Response: {"desc": "В статье представлен WorkFactory — масштабируемый фреймворк для автоматического создания согласованных и исполнимых профессиональных задач, которые используются для supervised fine-tuning LLM-агентов. Сначала строится information support graph, отражающий зависимости и источники необходимой информации, затем probe-запуски выявляют дефекты и запускают процедуру repair. На основе 112 686 задач из 612 профессий получены траектории учителя, которыми дообучены модели Qwen на 4B, 27B и 35B-A3B, образовавшие семейство WorkPilot. Все модели превосходят базовые на четырёх бенчмарках профессиональной работы, например WorkPilot-35B-A3B повышает Elo на GDPval-AA v2.1 с 879 до 1565.", "emoji": "🧑‍💼", "title": "Качественные задачи — основа умных AI-агентов"}

Abstract L3G-World uses a compact latent 3D Gaussian memory and alternating autoregressive updates with a video diffusion transformer to generate spatially consistent interactive worlds in real time.  Generated by thinkingmachines/Inkling-Small Interactive world models generate visual content conditioned on user actions, yet maintaining spatial consistency over long-horizon exploration in real time remains challenging. Existing approaches rely on either temporal context or explicit 3D memory, both of which scale poorly because memory grows with the length of the generated stream. Moreover, explicit 3D memories condition latent video generators by re-encoding RGB renderings, which precludes real-time generation. In this paper, we introduce a Latent 3D Gaussian Memory (L3G) that combines the efficiency of feed-forward reconstruction with a compact, rapidly renderable scene representation. Building on this memory, we propose L3G-World, in which a memory updater and a video diffusion transformer (DiT) alternate autoregressively to generate spatially consistent worlds. The memory updater spawns Gaussians in newly observed regions and refines existing ones, so that the memory grows with scene extent rather than stream length. Each Gaussian further stores a VAE latent feature, allowing the memory to be rendered directly into the DiT's latent space without VAE re-encoding. Experiments show that L3G-World achieves state-of-the-art generation quality and long-horizon spatial consistency, with up to 38.6times fewer 3D primitives, 3.2times faster memory updates, and 359times faster memory rendering relative to the most efficient prior 3D-memory baseline for each metric, enabling real-time world generation at 9 FPS.

Ключевая идея — компактная латентная память из 3D-гауссиан (Latent 3D Gaussian Memory), где каждый гауссиан хранит VAE-признаки и рендерится напрямую в латентное пространство DiT без повторного кодирования VAE. Память-апдейтер и video diffusion transformer работают в авторегрессионном чередовании: память растёт с размером сцены, а не с длиной потока. По результатам экспериментов модель достигает state-of-the-art качества, использует до 38.6 раз меньше 3D-примитивов и обеспечивает генерацию в реальном времени на уровне 9 FPS.", "emoji": "🌍", "title": "Согласованные миры в реальном времени благодаря компактной 3D-памяти"}

Abstract Retrieval heads remain sparse and shared across audio-visual modalities, and optimizing them improves document retrieval performance in multimodal models.  Generated by thinkingmachines/Inkling-Small Locating relevant evidence in long inputs is a core capability of Multimodal Large Language Models. Prior studies attribute this capability to retrieval heads, a sparse set of attention heads that transfer information from the context to the output. However, these analyses are confined to a limited set of modalities and the corresponding models. Moreover, how retrieval heads can be leveraged to enhance the model calls for further exploration. In this paper, we ask whether this mechanism expands together with the input modalities of the model. Taking audio-visual input as a newly added modality, we construct paired inputs in which the same answer is either spoken or shown on screen. With the evidence content fixed, retrieval heads remain sparse in the new modality and are shared to a large extent across modalities. Across 18 language, vision-language, and omni-modal checkpoints, omni-modal models largely preserve the retrieval heads of their backbones. Ablating these heads eliminates nearly all audio-visual retrieval. The heads shared across modalities make up fewer than 2\% of all heads, yet ablating them also degrades document understanding, spatial perception, and visual mathematics. Building on these findings, we turn retrieval heads into a target of optimization by training their attention with a ranking objective. This raises the page Recall@1 of Qwen3-VL-8B on MMDocIR by 6.03 points and improves document retrieval across three model families. Functional attention heads can thus serve both as a lens on multimodal models and as a target for improving them.
[30.10.2026 09:26] Response: {"desc": "Статья исследует «retrieval heads» — редкие attention heads, которые переносят релевантную информацию из длинного контекста в ответ Multimodal LLM. Авторы добавляют аудио-визуальную модальность и показывают, что эти головы остаются разреженными и в основном общими для разных модальностей, а у omni-modal моделей сохраняются из backbone. Абляция этих голов почти полностью убирает аудио-визуальный retrieval и ухудшает понимание документов, пространственное восприятие и визуальную математику. Обучение attention таких голов с ranking objective повышает Recall@1 Qwen3-VL-8B на MMDocIR на 6.03 пункта и улучшает document retrieval у трёх семейств моделей.", "emoji": "🔍", "title": "Retrieval heads: от инструмента анализа к цели оптимизации мультимодальных моделей"}

Abstract  Evaluating human-AI co-design requires testing whether coding agents can translate real users' multimodal requests into working application revisions. We introduce Co-Design Bench, a reference-free benchmark of 273 tasks curated from 37,463 deduplicated queries across 26,314 real user coding sessions. Its two tracks, Multimodal Editing (ME) and Repository Editing (RE), cover localized edits with optional image, audio, video, or document inputs and coordinated repository revisions. Tasks span three capability dimensions: Grounded Understanding, UI Transformation, and Functional Implementation. The tasks pair normalized requests and runnable pre-edit applications with 2,331 hidden expert-authored acceptance criteria. Our judge-then-refute protocol grades criteria using source and browser evidence, challenges positive verdicts with a separate LLM verifier, and audits visual and interaction claims without gold code or target screenshots. Under a shared agentic harness, the strongest of ten model endpoints passes an average of 69.9% of criteria per task but fully satisfies only 26.4% of tasks. All ten endpoints achieve lower criterion completion on repository edits spanning three or four change types than on those spanning one or two. Co-Design Bench provides a testbed for studying the gap between partial requirement satisfaction and complete task success under agentic execution for real-user application revisions.
[30.10.2026 09:27] Response: {"desc": "В статье представлен Co-Design Bench — новый бенчмарк для оценки того, насколько AI-агенты для написания кода способны превращать мультимодальные запросы реальных пользователей (текст, изображения, аудио, видео, документы) в работающие правки приложений. Набор включает 273 задачи из почти 26 тысяч реальных сессий и делится на два трека: локальное редактирование (Multimodal Editing) и согласованные изменения в репозитории (Repository Editing). Оценка проводится без эталонного кода: 2 331 скрытый критерий приёмки проверяется протоколом judge-then-refute, где положительные вердикты оспаривает отдельный LLM-верификатор. Результаты показывают, что лучшая из десяти моделей выполняет в среднем 69.9% критериев, но полностью решает лишь 26.4% задач, что выявляет разрыв между частичным и полным успехом агентного выполнения.", "emoji": "🧩", "title": "От частичного успеха к полной готовности: разрыв в AI-агентах для со-дизайна"}

Paper text to classify:

"Abstract  Evaluating human-AI co-design requires testing whether coding agents can translate real users' multimodal requests into working application revisions. We introduce Co-Design Bench, a reference-free benchmark of 273 tasks curated from 37,463 deduplicated queries across 26,314 real user coding sessions. Its two tracks, Multimodal Editing (ME) and Repository Editing (RE), cover localized edits with optional image, audio, video, or document inputs and coordinated repository revisions. Tasks span three capability dimensions: Grounded Understanding, UI Transformation, and Functional Implementation. The tasks pair normalized requests and runnable pre-edit applications with 2,331 hidden expert-authored acceptance criteria. Our judge-then-refute protocol grades criteria using source and browser evidence, challenges positive verdicts with a separate LLM verifier, and audits visual and interaction claims without gold code or target screenshots. Under a shared agentic harness, the strongest of ten model endpoints passes an average of 69.9% of criteria per task but fully satisfies only 26.4% of tasks. All ten endpoints achieve lower criterion completion on repository edits spanning three or four change types than on those spanning one or two. Co-Design Bench provides a testbed for studying the gap between partial requirement satisfaction and complete task success under agentic execution for real-user application revisions."

Abstract VibeGame is a framework that uses specialized adversarial agents and an AI-native engine to build complete 2D games from natural-language prompts with interactive validation and self-evolving reusable components.  Generated by thinkingmachines/Inkling-Small Large language model agents have enabled software development through natural-language interaction, but extending this paradigm to end-to-end game development remains challenging. Building a playable game requires coordinating code, scenes, assets, animations, physics, and configurations, while allowing developers to validate their integrated behavior through interactive testing. We introduce VibeGame, a framework for creating complete 2D game projects from natural-language prompts and optional reference images. Its AI-native game engine represents project state as schema-validated structured text and provides frame-synchronous runtime control, enabling agents to inspect game states, execute actions, and verify behavior at their own pace. Built upon this interface, we design an Adversarial Agent Team of eight specialized agents to separate implementation from review, so that problems are found and fixed through multi-round loops. For each conducted and accepted project, we further introduce a training-free self-evolution mechanism to distill it into reusable skeletons, modules, and collaboration contracts for future development. We evaluate prompt-assets-to-game (PA2G), where agents are given an asset library, and prompt-to-game (P2G), where agents generate their own art. On a GameCraft-Bench subset, VibeGame in P2G consistently outperforms a range of agent harnesses in either P2G or PA2G with the same underlying model, including GPT-6.1-Sol.

Ключевая особенность — AI-native движок, который хранит состояние проекта в виде структурированного текста с валидацией по схеме и даёт агентам покадровое управление рантаймом для проверки поведения. Команда из восьми специализированных агентов разделяет роли реализации и ревью, находя и исправляя ошибки в многораундовых циклах. Abstract HairDeck generates stylized hair cards and spring-bone animations as unordered token sets using a diffusion transformer, improving accuracy and speed over prior methods.  Generated by thinkingmachines/Inkling-Small The hair of stylized characters in games and avatars is built from hair cards: artist-placed ribbons whose geometry is the visible hair, animated by spring bones. Existing image-based methods produce realistic hair or a single fused mesh, while CHARM, the only generator of stylized hair cards, decodes a hairstyle autoregressively as a long sequence of control points. Observing that a card hairstyle is an unordered set of artist-defined primitives, we propose HairDeck, which generates it as one: each card is a single token, and a set diffusion transformer denoises all cards together. From a single image, HairDeck generates the cards and the spring-bone chains that drive them, yielding an animatable asset that exports to ready-to-use downstream formats. On CHARM's own test set, it lowers Chamfer distance by 44% and card-orientation error by more than half while running 33 times faster, and from single images it outperforms CHARM, image-to-3D pipelines and strand-based methods. Project page: https://hyzcluster.github.io/HairDeck
[30.10.2026 09:27] Response: {"desc": "HairDeck — это метод, который по одному изображению генерирует стилизованные hair cards (ленты волос) и spring-bone цепочки для их анимации. В отличие от CHARM, который декодирует причёску авторегрессивно как длинную последовательность control points, HairDeck рассматривает причёску как неупорядоченное множество карточек, где каждая карточка представлена одним токеном. Все карточки одновременно денойзит set diffusion transformer, что повышает точность и ускоряет генерацию примерно в 33 раза. Результатом становится готовый к анимации ассет, который экспортируется в стандартные форматы для игр и аватаров.", "emoji": "💇", "title": "Причёска целиком: быстрее и точнее через диффузию над множеством карточек"}

Abstract WeVisCut is a script-to-video system that trains a compact policy to coordinate video editing tools, recover from failures, and reduce token usage while maintaining quality.  Generated by thinkingmachines/Inkling-Small Producing long-form videos from narration requires interdependent decisions that remain coherent across shots. Existing agentic editors often use multi-stage workflows driven by general models, leaving the quality-cost trade-off of compact, task-adapted policies unclear. We address this gap with WeVisCut, a long-form script-to-video system that connects tool execution with policy learning. Its agent harness maintains shared production state so that tool feedback informs subsequent decisions and repairs. Because finished videos do not expose these decisions, our four-stage data pipeline combines editing inversion with verified replay to turn recovered editing intent into executable supervision. Applied to 3,750 finished videos, this pipeline yields over 130K training examples. We use this supervision to train a single 9B policy that coordinates production and recovers from failures within the same harness. Moreover, we introduce a 100-task benchmark and five metrics assessing editing quality and delivery reliability. Under a shared tool environment, WeVisCut substantially outperforms its base model and achieves comparable performance to Claude Opus 5 while using nearly 33x fewer generated tokens per successful output minute. It also outperforms task-matched native editors on our proposed metrics.

Авторы строят четырехэтапный пайплайн данных, который восстанавливает намерения монтажа из готовых видео (editing inversion) и проверяет их через replay, получая более 130 тысяч обучающих примеров из 3750 видео. Abstract IntReal introduces a real-world interleaved multimodal dataset and evaluation framework to diagnose unified model strengths and improve generation through targeted training.  Generated by thinkingmachines/Inkling-Small Humans routinely consume multimodal content, where textual and visual information is naturally interleaved. Although Unified Multimodal Models (UMMs) can jointly generate text and images, multimodal interleaved generation remains largely underexplored. Progress in this area is hindered by two key limitations: (1) the lack of well-defined interleaved generation tasks and corresponding training data, and (2) the absence of fine-grained evaluation protocols that can reliably diagnose model performance across different capability dimensions. To this end, we present IntReal, comprising two complementary components: IntReal-100K, a real-world dataset of multimodal interleaved samples spanning four major categories, and the IntReal Evaluation Suite, which evaluates model performance across seven complementary dimensions using specialized expert models, enabling coarse-to-fine diagnosis. Extensive experiments validate our evaluation suite through low inter-metric redundancy (max pairwise absolute Spearman's ρleq 0.42 ), high agreement with human judgments (83.3% accuracy), and strong consistency across different expert evaluators (Spearman's ρ> 0.9 ), supporting the complementarity and reliability of our metrics. By benchmarking four families of UMMs, we show that IntReal Evaluation Suite reveals distinct strengths and weaknesses across models. Post-training experiments demonstrate the utility of IntReal-100K for improving interleaved generation and further show that training unimodal generation can also benefit interleaved generation. These findings suggest that interleaved generation can benefit from modality-specific optimization, rather than requiring joint text-image training at every stage.

Оценка включает семь взаимодополняющих метрик, которые используют специализированные экспертные модели, и показывает низкую избыточность, высокое согласие с человеческими оценками (83.3%) и устойчивость между разными экспертами. Generated by thinkingmachines/Inkling-Small Web agents require diverse, executable, and resettable website environments for reproducible training and evaluation. Human-assisted mirrors demand per-site engineering, while generative construction can diverge from specified real websites. Capture-and-replay preserves observations, with interaction coverage constrained by captured content and replay capabilities.   We present WebAnything, a fully automated framework that reconstructs source-grounded, reusable Web environments from real URLs without per-site manual implementation or repair after initial setup and authorization. An Explorer-Builder collects browser-observable structures, resources, and interaction outcomes; an independent verifier checks frozen candidates and guides local repair or targeted source recapture.   We construct 244 website artifacts across 14 application domains and 53 categories, supporting 12,984 executed and accepted tasks. Compared with human-assisted WebHarbor, WebAnything achieves higher aggregate scores on three fidelity metrics, with 12 wins, two ties, and no losses in a blind audit. Fine-tuning Qwen3.5-9B on 5,856 interaction trajectories improves REAL success by 9.9 percentage points, Odysseys-Easy Rubric Avg and Task Avg by 6.25 and 6.32 percentage points, and WebArena-Verified Overall success by 13.33 percentage points under the fixed harness. Code: https://github.com/china-qijizhifeng/Web-Anything

Abstract SpecSlot improves pixel-space generative Transformers by using localized spectral slots and restricted global attention to increase image detail without raising computational cost.  Generated by thinkingmachines/Inkling-Small Pixel-space generative Transformers typically use one token per image patch, so capturing finer detail requires more tokens and makes global attention increasingly expensive. We observe that finer detail does not necessarily require more global communication: low-frequency structure benefits from global exchange, while high-frequency detail can be modeled locally. Based on this insight, we introduce SpecSlot, a pixel-space rectified-flow Transformer that decouples output detail from the length of the global attention sequence. SpecSlot expands each spatial anchor into multiple slots, each assigned a disjoint band of DCT coefficients; this separates anchor density from spectral subdivision. Only the lowest-frequency slot from each anchor participates in global attention, while all slots interact locally. This allows the model to increase spectral detail without enlarging the global sequence. A spatial head further adds a local correction to the spectral prediction, while the generative state, target, and loss remain in pixel space. On ImageNet-256, SpecSlot achieves a comparable final FID to a FLOPs-matched JiT control while converging faster. On ImageNet-512, it improves FID by 2.84 over the control with 3.9% fewer FLOPs, and comes within only 0.69 FID of JiT-B/16 at 38.2% lower cost. On text-to-image generation, SpecSlot surpasses JiT-B/8 on DPG-Bench by 1.06 at 40.6% lower forward cost.
[30.10.2026 09:28] Response: {"desc": "В статье представлен SpecSlot — pixel-space трансформер для генерации изображений на основе rectified flow, который повышает детализацию без роста вычислительных затрат. Ключевая идея: низкочастотная структура требует глобального взаимодействия через attention, а высокочастотные детали можно моделировать локально. Каждый пространственный якорь разбивается на несколько слотов, каждый из которых отвечает за свою полосу DCT-коэффициентов, и только самый низкочастотный слот участвует в глобальном attention. На ImageNet-256 и ImageNet-512 SpecSlot сопоставим или превосходит контрольную модель JiT по FID при меньшем количестве FLOPs, а в задаче text-to-image также показывает лучший результат на DPG-Bench.", "emoji": "🧩", "title": "Детали без лишней глобальной связи: больше разрешения, та же цена"}

Abstract Glance integrates reranking and generation into a single backbone using lightweight adapters and pointer heads to score passages from shared context, reducing memory overhead while improving retrieval and answer quality.  Generated by thinkingmachines/Inkling-Small Retrieval-augmented generation commonly serves separate models for reranking and answering, duplicating model residency and passage processing. We introduce Glance, which equips the generator with a lightweight decision adapter and pointer head to score candidate passages from a shared prefill. Short relevance probes branch from the cached context, producing one score per passage without separately prefilling each query-passage pair. A single resident 9B backbone supports generation, general decisions and reranking, with approximately 0.1 GiB of additional memory per decision skill. After 39 minutes of MS MARCO fine-tuning, Glance achieves nDCG@10 of 0.741 on SciFact and 0.345 on NFCorpus, compared with 0.721 and 0.326 for bge-reranker-v2-m3. On 300 HotpotQA questions, its selected context improves answer F1 by 0.045 over cross-encoder-selected context under the same base generator (paired bootstrap 95% CI [0.014, 0.076]). In a scaling study, reranking quality keeps rising up to 200 candidates (43k tokens) scored from one shared prefill, where it exceeds the cross-encoder by 0.058 nDCG@10, and a probe-only activated adapter lets generation continue from the original passage cache. These initial results demonstrate generator-based reranking with one resident backbone; evaluation currently covers two retrieval datasets, one QA dataset and one backbone family.
[30.10.2026 09:28] Response: {"desc": "Статья представляет Glance — подход, который объединяет переранжирование и генерацию ответов в одной языковой модели (LLM) с помощью лёгких адаптеров и pointer head. Модель оценивает кандидатные пассажи по общему кэшу контекста, поэтому не нужно отдельно обрабатывать каждую пару запрос-пассаж, что снижает потребление памяти. Такой подход на бенчмарках SciFact, NFCorpus и HotpotQA даёт лучшее качество поиска и ответов, чем отдельные cross-encoder модели. Авторы отмечают, что эксперименты пока ограничены двумя наборами данных для поиска, одним QA-датасетом и одним семейством моделей.", "emoji": "🔍", "title": "Один backbone для поиска и генерации ответов"}

We introduce Glance, which equips the generator with a lightweight decision adapter and pointer head to score candidate passages from a shared prefill. Short relevance probes branch from the cached context, producing one score per passage without separately prefilling each query-passage pair. A single resident 9B backbone supports generation, general decisions and reranking, with approximately 0.1 GiB of additional memory per decision skill. After 39 minutes of MS MARCO fine-tuning, Glance achieves nDCG@10 of 0.741 on SciFact and 0.345 on NFCorpus, compared with 0.721 and 0.326 for bge-reranker-v2-m3. On 300 HotpotQA questions, its selected context improves answer F1 by 0.045 over cross-encoder-selected context under the same base generator (paired bootstrap 95% CI [0.014, 0.076]). In a scaling study, reranking quality keeps rising up to 200 candidates (43k tokens) scored from one shared prefill, where it exceeds the cross-encoder by 0.058 nDCG@10, and a probe-only activated adapter lets generation continue from the original passage cache. These initial results demonstrate generator-based reranking with one resident backbone; evaluation currently covers two retrieval datasets, one QA dataset and one backbone family."

Abstract Proactive video understanding should include asking for missing information under uncertainty, and the AskStream benchmark reveals current models struggle with this decision.  Generated by thinkingmachines/Inkling-Small Recent advances in multimodal large language models have increased interest in interaction paradigms for video understanding. The evolution from offline to streaming and proactive interaction has gradually shifted models from passive responders toward more proactive agents that can decide when to answer. However, existing proactive paradigms typically assume that the information required to answer a query is complete, leaving models to only wait or answer. In the real world, however, information is often incomplete due to uncertainty from either the video or the user. In proactive paradigms, such uncertainty can cause the model to wait forever or generate highly hallucinatory responses. We therefore argue that proactive interaction should be redefined with a third action: ask, allowing models to actively ask for missing information. To study when and what models should ask before answering, we introduce AskStream, the first multi-turn benchmark spanning video and user uncertainty for streaming video understanding, with four task categories and eight subtasks. It contains 438 videos with 2,714 manually annotated frame-level time windows across three types: wait, ask, and answer. Across both multiple-choice and open-ended settings, even the strongest model attains only 52.18% on our proposed Ask-F1 metric, highlighting substantial limitations in proactive information seeking under uncertainty.
[30.10.2026 09:28] Response: {"desc": "Статья вводит AskStream — первый многоходовой бенчмарк для потоковой обработки видео, где модели должны решать, ждать, спрашивать или отвечать. Авторы показывают, что существующие мультимодальные LLM рассчитывают на полную информацию и поэтому либо зависают в ожидании, либо галлюцинируют. Предлагается третье действие — ask, позволяющее модели активно запрашивать недостающие данные у пользователя. Лучшая из протестированных моделей достигает лишь 52.18% по метрике Ask-F1, что указывает на серьезные ограничения в проактивном поиске информации при неопределенности.", "emoji": "🎬", "title": "Модели должны уметь спрашивать, а не только отвечать"}

Abstract EditAgent broadens agentic image editing by routing steps to diverse editors and using verification records for listwise judging, improving reliability and efficiency.  Generated by thinkingmachines/Inkling-Small Agentic image editors decompose complex requests into steps, but when a step fails, they mostly retry with the same narrow set of tools and a judge that scores each output by eye directly. We argue that such agents are bounded by two spaces: the action space of what the agent can do, and the observation space of what its judge can see. On our expert-annotated benchmark, a general-purpose editor yields no usable result in over 40% of the cases, and no judge is reliable across edit types. We present EditAgent, which expands both spaces. It routes each step to general editors and specialized methods, and attaches to each candidate a verification record. With these records, off-the-shelf VLM judges outperform finetuned reward models. Candidates are generated in parallel and judged in one listwise call: experts prefer this to sequential refinement in 78.5% of decided comparisons, at no more than half the judge calls. EditAgent improves two open-weight editors on three benchmarks and serves as a data engine for training stronger editors.
[30.10.2026 09:28] Response: {"desc": "Статья представляет EditAgent — агентную систему для редактирования изображений, которая расширяет пространство действий, направляя каждый шаг к разным редакторам, включая специализированные методы. Каждому кандидату прикрепляется запись верификации, что позволяет готовым VLM-судьям превосходить дообученные reward-модели. Кандидаты генерируются параллельно и оцениваются одним listwise-вызовом, что эксперты предпочитают последовательному уточнению в 78.5% случаев при вдвое меньшем числе вызовов судьи. Система улучшает два open-weight редактора на трёх бенчмарках и служит источником данных для обучения более сильных редакторов.", "emoji": "🖼️", "title": "Расширяем действия и зрение агента — надёжнее редактирование изображений"}

Abstract Investor's Last Exam evaluates financial agents on end-to-end equity research using frozen evidence, expert criteria, and dependency graphs, revealing that top models excel at research but fail at forecasting and investment judgments.  Generated by thinkingmachines/Inkling-Small Financial agents must form forecasts and investment judgments from decision-time evidence, with predictions evaluated against subsequent outcomes. Existing benchmarks assess financial knowledge, research workflows, or forecasting, but evaluating the full research process requires a strictly frozen information set and professional evaluation criteria. We introduce Investor's Last Exam (ILE), an equity-research benchmark led by experienced buy-side investors and fund managers. Its three components are a versioned financial database spanning 3.43 million document versions and nine source categories, supplemented by structured market data, with validated timestamps governing retrieval and computation; a task taxonomy organized around professional analyst deliverables; and an expert-led framework linking scoring criteria to sources, calculations, and assumptions through financial dependency graphs. Evaluation spans 86 assignments and jointly assesses research quality, forecast accuracy, and simulated investment performance. GPT-6 Astra and Claude Opus 5.5 lead overall at 68.1 and 66.9 out of 100, yet even Astra scores only 52.5 on Forecast & Model. Our results show that even state-of-the-art models fail to translate overall research capability into predictive performance. ILE shifts the evaluation unit from isolated capabilities to the evidence--assumption--judgment chain, allowing researchers to locate where analytical advantages fail to translate into forecasts and investment judgments, and providing a traceable, diagnostic foundation for training and evaluating financial agents.

Abstract CoSTA decouples generation and synthesis token spaces to accelerate spoken dialogue models while preserving quality and compatibility.  Generated by thinkingmachines/Inkling-Small Spoken Dialogue Models (SDMs) typically share a speech-token space for generation and synthesis, leading to long autoregressive sequences and costly inference. Lower-rate speech tokenizers shorten sequences but may introduce compression-quality trade-offs and costly replacement without breaking this coupling. To address this limitation, we propose CoSTA, a compatibility-preserving post-hoc framework that decouples generation and synthesis token spaces while retaining the original speech tokenizer and decoder. CoSTA extends the generation token space with compressed representations via mean-initialized vocabulary extension, pronunciation adaptation, and dialogue adaptation, followed by deterministic expansion back to the original speech-token space before synthesis. On bilingual speech-dialogue benchmarks, CoSTA achieves approximately 1.7times expanded speech-token generation speed, lower full-response latency, and higher throughput while preserving response quality. Analyses show more sustained interaction under limited budgets and a practical trade-off among compression, decoding efficiency, and quality. Further experiments demonstrate cross-architecture transfer and complementarity to low-rate tokenization.
[30.10.2026 09:28] Response: {"desc": "Статья предлагает CoSTA — пост-хок фреймворк, который разделяет пространства токенов для генерации и синтеза речи в Spoken Dialogue Models. Генерация работает с сжатыми представлениями, расширяя словарь через mean-initialized vocabulary extension, адаптацию произношения и адаптацию диалога. Перед синтезом сжатые токены детерминированно разворачиваются обратно в исходное пространство речевых токенов, поэтому оригинальный токенизатор и декодер сохраняются. Эксперименты на двуязычных речевых диалоговых бенчмарках показывают примерно 1.7-кратное ускорение генерации, снижение задержки и рост пропускной способности без потери качества ответов.", "emoji": "🗣️", "title": "Быстрее говорить, сохраняя качество: разделяем токены генерации и синтеза речи"}

Abstract AB-Attention approximates dense video diffusion attention with high sparsity via intra-block clustering, weighted sampling, and fine-grained kernel gathering, improving fidelity and speed.  Generated by thinkingmachines/Inkling-Small Video diffusion transformers generate high-quality videos, but the quadratic cost of 3D full attention dominates their inference. Dynamic block-sparse attention reduces this cost by computing exact attention only on key/value blocks selected from current activations. However, its fidelity degrades under aggressive sparsity: blocks are scored from coarse summaries or formed by costly global clustering, pruned blocks vanish from the softmax, and whole tiles are kept or dropped even when only a few of their keys matter. To address these limitations, we propose Accurate Block Attention (AB-Attention), a training-free method that accurately approximates dense attention at high sparsity. Its Intra-Block Clustering Selector (IBCS) estimates block importance faithfully yet cheaply by clustering queries only within each query block, without permuting tokens. Sampling with Inclusion-Probability Weighting (SIP) then samples blocks in proportion to these estimates and reweights them by inverse inclusion probability, so that one pass estimates dense attention rather than truncating it. Finally, its kernel gathers key/value blocks much smaller than a tile into full tensor-core tiles, refining selection at no extra tensor-core cost. On Wan2.1 and HunyuanVideo, AB-Attention skips 89--92\% of attention, whereas the baselines compute 1.8--3.6times as much, yet it still improves PSNR by up to 4.4~dB over the strongest baseline and achieves up to 2.23times end-to-end speedup. Our code is available at https://github.com/InfRec-Cardinal/AB-Attention.

Ключевые компоненты: IBCS оценивает важность блоков через кластеризацию запросов внутри каждого блока, а SIP выбирает блоки с вероятностями включения и применяет обратное взвешивание, чтобы оценка оставалась несмещённой. Ядро (kernel) собирает небольшие key/value блоки в полные тензорные тайлы, уточняя выбор без дополнительных затрат на tensor cores. На моделях Wan2.1 и HunyuanVideo метод пропускает 89–92% вычислений внимания, повышает PSNR до 4.4 dB и ускоряет inference до 2.23 раза.", "emoji": "🎬", "title": "Быстрое внимание для видео без потери качества"}

Paper text to classify:

"Abstract AB-Attention approximates dense video diffusion attention with high sparsity via intra-block clustering, weighted sampling, and fine-grained kernel gathering, improving fidelity and speed.  Generated by thinkingmachines/Inkling-Small Video diffusion transformers generate high-quality videos, but the quadratic cost of 3D full attention dominates their inference. Dynamic block-sparse attention reduces this cost by computing exact attention only on key/value blocks selected from current activations. However, its fidelity degrades under aggressive sparsity: blocks are scored from coarse summaries or formed by costly global clustering, pruned blocks vanish from the softmax, and whole tiles are kept or dropped even when only a few of their keys matter. To address these limitations, we propose Accurate Block Attention (AB-Attention), a training-free method that accurately approximates dense attention at high sparsity. Its Intra-Block Clustering Selector (IBCS) estimates block importance faithfully yet cheaply by clustering queries only within each query block, without permuting tokens. Sampling with Inclusion-Probability Weighting (SIP) then samples blocks in proportion to these estimates and reweights them by inverse inclusion probability, so that one pass estimates dense attention rather than truncating it. Finally, its kernel gathers key/value blocks much smaller than a tile into full tensor-core tiles, refining selection at no extra tensor-core cost. On Wan2.1 and HunyuanVideo, AB-Attention skips 89--92\% of attention, whereas the baselines compute 1.8--3.6times as much, yet it still improves PSNR by up to 4.4~dB over the strongest baseline and achieves up to 2.23times end-to-end speedup. Our code is available at https://github.com/InfRec-Cardinal/AB-Attention."

[30.10.2026 09:29] Claude request. Model: claude-haiku-5-5. Prompt: Read an abstract of the ML paper and return a JSON with fields: 'desc': explanation of the paper in Russian (4 sentences), use correct machine learning terms. 'emoji': emoji that will reflect the theme of an article somehow, only one emoji. 'title': a slogan of a main idea of the article in Russian. Return only JSON and nothing else.

Abstract Soft-MTP removes a noisy gradient residual from multi-token prediction via a straight-through KL recovery trick, improving perplexity, speculative decoding, and reasoning without added cost.  Generated by thinkingmachines/Inkling-Small Multi-Token Prediction (MTP) is now a common ingredient in language-model pre-training, as it improves downstream performance and enables faster inference through speculative decoding. We identify a flaw in standard MTP training, in which a residual component of the MTP gradient interferes with both next-token and multi-token learning, increasingly so with scale. Specifically, the MTP cross-entropy gradient can be decomposed exactly into a KL self-distillation term that pulls each MTP head toward the model's own next-token prediction (NTP) distribution and improves speculative decoding, and a residual that has a low signal-to-noise ratio and buries the useful KL signal. We introduce the KL gradient-recovery trick: when two distributions share a linear output head, the gradient of their KL divergence can be recovered exactly by combining their cross-entropy gradients through straight-through gradient routing. Unlike minimizing the KL directly, this trick requires no vocabulary-sized logit matrices and is compatible with standard fused cross-entropy kernels. This enables Soft Multi-Token Prediction (Soft-MTP), a one-line code change to MTP that removes the noisy residual and backpropagates only the KL self-distillation signal by reusing the existing NTP and MTP cross-entropy computations. Soft-MTP supports both DeepSeek- and Meta-style MTP, adds no parameters, and incurs no measurable overhead. From 167M to 1.2B parameters, Soft-MTP improves over standard MTP with lower NTP perplexity, faster speculative decoding and higher zero-shot accuracy at every scale, lower MTP perplexity from 689M onward, and stronger math and code reasoning after fine-tuning at 1.2B. Soft-MTP also matches, if not exceeds, direct KL divergence minimization on most metrics. Soft-MTP is, in effect, a free lunch for MTP optimization. Our code can be found at https://github.com/JaydenTeoh/Soft-MTP.
[30.10.2026 09:29] Response: {"desc": "В статье обнаружен недостаток стандартного обучения Multi-Token Prediction (MTP): часть градиента cross-entropy мешает как обучению предсказания следующего токена, так и многотокенного предсказания, и этот эффект усиливается с ростом масштаба модели. Авторы показывают, что градиент MTP раскладывается на KL self-distillation, полезный для speculative decoding, и шумный остаточный член с низким signal-to-noise ratio. Предложенный трюк KL gradient-recovery через straight-through gradient routing позволяет получить градиент KL-дивергенции без больших матриц логитов, а метод Soft-MTP реализуется одной строкой кода. Abstract An online reinforcement learning framework trains UI navigation agents on live mobile apps using a planning agent, UI grounding model, and a novel neighbor-conditioned value estimation method to improve robustness and reduce inference costs.  Generated by thinkingmachines/Inkling-Small While frontier vision-language models demonstrate strong capabilities in user interface (UI) navigation, their high inference costs prohibit everyday use. Conversely, smaller models trained via supervised fine-tuning (SFT) or offline reinforcement learning often memorize static trajectories, leading to brittle execution and an inability to recover from errors. To address these limitations, we propose a novel online reinforcement learning framework that trains UI navigation agents directly on live iOS applications. Our framework decouples the agent into two specialized components: a Planning agent and a UI Grounding model. We rely on an LLM-as-a-Judge to provide comprehensive reward signals, combining dense, step-level feedback to evaluate intermediate progress with a terminal reward for overall task completion. To effectively leverage this dual reward structure, we optimize the agent using Proximal Policy Optimization (PPO) with step-level advantage estimation. Furthermore, we introduce GiGPPO, a novel neighbor-conditioned value estimation method that improves critic stability by leveraging reward signals from visually similar states across concurrent trajectories. To support this online training paradigm at scale, we introduce an efficient infrastructure that orchestrates hundreds of concurrent virtual iOS devices. Evaluations on rigorous benchmarks, including UINavBench, iOSWorld, and AndroidWorld, demonstrate that our online RL trained agents substantially outperform both SFT and terminal-only RL baselines while accelerating training convergence. Notably, our 8B RL agents achieve nearly a sevenfold improvement over the SFT baseline in out-of-distribution generalization to Android environments, while outperforming Gemini-3.5-Flash and tying with Gemini-3.1-Pro on real iOS applications.
[30.10.2026 09:29] Response: {"desc": "Статья предлагает онлайн-фреймворк обучения с подкреплением для агентов, которые навигируют по интерфейсам мобильных приложений, разделяя агента на Planning agent и UI Grounding model. Вместо offline RL или SFT, которые приводят к запоминанию траекторий, агенты обучаются на живых iOS-приложениях с помощью PPO, а награда формируется LLM-as-a-Judge: плотные пошаговые сигналы плюс терминальная награда. Ключевой вклад — GiGPPO, метод оценки value с учётом соседних визуально похожих состояний из параллельных траекторий, что стабилизирует critic. Восьмимиллиардные RL-агенты заметно превосходят SFT-базовые модели и хорошо обобщаются на Android, сопоставимы с крупными моделями Gemini на iOS.", "emoji": "📱", "title": "Учимся навигировать по приложениям на живых устройствах, а не по запомненным траекториям"}
